## Cache-routing terminal binding — 2026-10-28

The prepared trusted command gives the existing cache fixture a fresh stage-owned
root. Before publishing its report, stage can check the fixture's native launch,
terminal and raw output, compare its copied `bin/run.ts` to the execution-image
Source digest, and bind the observation to the validated coverage run. Missing,
aliased, mismatched, timed-out or UNKNOWN evidence refuses publication. This
does not activate the trusted stage or prove arbitrary descendant containment.

The existing owner ran the real invalid direct invocation on the changed test:
1 pass, 8 filtered, 2.81 seconds total, with complete capture and observed owned
group closure. Its direct child exited 1 with the expected blocked result;
the copied `run.ts` SHA-256 matched the selected current Source. Preserve
`/workspace/contour-cache-binding-RpPWTA2q/`, including its fixture and native
receipts. This target is not a coverage run or total-stage timing measurement.
The assertion REDs are retained at `/workspace/contour-cache-root-red-mD7CAmLx`
and `/workspace/contour-cache-receipt-red-rMYcZh1w`. The final grouped checks at
`/workspace/contour-cache-receipt-final-O48B3J1P` passed 7 tests and 103
expectations with complete capture and owned-group closure. Scoped strict (511
files), configured type-aware lint, formatting and independent read-only review
passed. Full coverage, timing headroom and the remaining known child effects
are not established by this package; old UNKNOWN resources are unchanged.

- Derived cache may be beside a copied bundle or under HOME for a node_modules
  root; custom paths and `0` remain explicit. Temp, HOME, cache, the actual

Read-only historical source tracing narrows, but does not resolve, that locator:

The current fixture observer does not yet supply a complete proof for this
selection. Do not promote this map or a targeted result into lane admission;

separate timing gate before a producer can advance.

## Trusted coverage child inventory boundary, 2026-10-27

The selected `run-entrypoint.test.ts` now contains two direct `Bun.spawn` calls
for the closed-output regressions added at `5e4d1526`. Each test retains its
child, uses a four-second timeout, intentionally cancels one pipe, consumes the
other and awaits the direct child's terminal result. Its inline program imports
`run.ts` as a library, so that program does not enter the cache re-execution
path. The tests' passing assertions demonstrate their direct terminal outcomes;
they do not publish separate fixture-child receipts or prove process-group
emptiness. The current fixture observer intercepts only Node child-process APIs
and is not active for the selected trusted lane. The new Bun launches therefore
belong in its reviewed effect/child inventory before admission. Do not treat the
older inventory's absence of `Bun.spawn` as current.

Changing these calls to Node `spawn` alone would not establish a receipt: the
observer is still dormant for this lane, and its stream proof requires `end`,
whereas these tests intentionally close a reader. That case needs an explicit
bounded, run-bound interpretation of the expected closed stream. The separate
cache re-execution still starts a detached process group that the outer CLI group
observation cannot see. No runtime or numeric qualification follows from this
inspection; preserve the existing UNKNOWN fences and full-stage HOLD.

## Bun stdout API control, 2026-10-27

The pinned Bun and its installed 1.4.4 types expose `Bun.write(Bun.stdout, ...)`;
the call returns the number of bytes. A single bounded control used the same
outer capture owner, Bun-parent `spawnSync` pipe, sandbox, child timeout and
`SA` payload as the prior small-output comparison. The child awaited that API
and rejected a count other than two. At
`/workspace/contour-bun-stdout-api-6dZWw9`, the child returned status 0,
stdout `SA`, empty stderr and no spawn error; the outer owner observed exit 0,
complete capture and the owned group gone. The probe did not alter permissions
or retry a denial. Its two-byte result proves neither the full CLI contract nor
the cause of the earlier stream-write EPERM. In particular it says nothing
about large output, partial writes, slow readers, broken pipes, Windows, other
CLI output paths, full-stage runtime or old UNKNOWN custody.

The installed `bun-types` 1.4.4 states that an `ArrayBufferView` stdout or
stderr is not implemented. Its documented `maxBuffer` says the child is killed
on exceeding the output cap; that text alone does not prove equivalence to the
existing Node `spawnSync` contract. Neither API changes coverage admission or
authorizes a capture replacement. Preserve old EPERM and UNKNOWN evidence.

## Parent runtime capture control, 2026-10-27

Attack-the-premise control: the existing `/workspace/contour-bun-issue-repro-JQ5W0V`
has a Bun parent for both children; its Node child does not control the parent
runtime. On Source `253ea9f3`, the same child payload and `spawnSync` pipe,
encoding and 4s child timeout were used under a Node 24.19.0 parent, with
explicit Bun 1.4.4+38afee6c8 and Node executable paths. The two new owned-group
attempts and native execution/capture records are retained in
`/workspace/contour-parent-runtime-0zAWDG`; each parent exited 0, capture was
complete and its owned group was observed gone. No old UNKNOWN was replayed.

The Node-parent/Bun-child attempt returned child status 1, stdout `S` and the
same Bun stdout-write EPERM callback; Node's `spawnSync` result also contained
an EPERM error. The Node-parent/Node-child attempt returned child status 0,
stdout `S`, callback `ok`, and a `spawnSync` EPERM error. The expected full
payload is `SA`; a successful callback does not establish delivery of `A`.
The existing Bun-parent controls likewise recorded Bun-child status 1/EPERM,
Node-child status 0/callback `ok`, and only `S` delivered for each. This bounded
matrix rejects a Bun-parent-only hypothesis; it does not isolate whether Bun,
the host transport, or their interaction causes the write failure. Node-parent
capture is not a qualified workaround. The old process/capture fences and full
coverage HOLD are unchanged.

## Explicit empty Bun cache, 2026-10-27

`pinnedEnvironment` treats an explicitly empty
`BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH` as absent and replaces it with a derived
path. [Bun documents](https://bun.com/docs/runtime/environment-variables#configuring-bun)
empty and `0` as cache-disable settings. A proposed change preserved the empty
value and checked the running pin and manifest without a re-execution. It is
**not adopted**: skipping `runPinnedBun` also skips its bounded child and can
allow work after an inherited deadline. A loop-free replacement must preserve
real timeout, signal, cleanup and native outcome semantics without accepting a
caller-forged marker as authority. Existing runtime behavior remains unchanged.

The retained red target `/workspace/contour-observer-target-uWDXha` reproduced
the incorrect derived path. Under the proposed patch, focused target
`/workspace/contour-observer-target-8D6tZc` passed both cache cases. Its real Bun child
imports a TypeScript file over 4 KiB: the custom-path control creates a `.pile`,
while the empty-path case leaves its private home and temp without one. Both
native child markers and the parent capture are retained. The scoped strict/lint
receipt is `/workspace/contour-observer-static-aj5RPT`. Independent blind review
identified the inherited-budget regression. The native targeted negative control
`/workspace/contour-observer-target-vvwbVE` confirmed execution continued after
`VIDA_PINNED_COMMAND_DEADLINE_MS=1`; native exit, capture and group were observed.
The unadopted proposal and added tests are retained at
`/workspace/contour-empty-cache-proposal-Ko5DtZtb/proposal.patch` against Source
`5b5d28e4`. No old counter is promoted or projection edited. The current
`bun-cli-run` full-proof HOLD, descendant custody and timing gates remain.
No full producer or cutover was run.

An independent review rejected unconditional re-execution with the same empty
cache setting: the child would take the same branch again. A possible marker-free
design is a bounded bootstrap that imports `run.ts` and invokes `main` with the
original arguments, while `runPinnedBun` retains timeout and cleanup ownership.
This changes the actual child argv and bypasses the direct-entrypoint help path.
It needs design approval and native controls for help, arguments, exit, signals,
deadline, cache, loaded-input receipts and the three focused CLI subjects before
implementation. It is a proposal, not a qualified successor or measured saving.

## Trusted coverage descendant boundary, 2026-10-27

At Source `c8003666`, `bin/run.ts` can re-execute Bun through `runPinnedBun`
when the entrypoint's cache namespace differs. `boundedSpawnSync` uses detached
process groups on POSIX. A direct test-child receipt alone therefore does not
account for this child's own subprocesses. The parent fixture observer currently
sees direct Node `spawn`/`spawnSync` calls only; selected CLI receipts and process
group observation do not by themselves prove that separately detached descendants
have terminated. The next implementation step must bind those actual nested
operations to supported custody, or retain UNKNOWN and HOLD. Do not prevent the
cache-routing scenario or replace its native contract merely to avoid the child.

A bounded blind review reused the completed historical run `8d7391ed`'s 181
loaded-input rows and found no textual `Bun.spawn` or `Deno.Command` calls in
those inputs. It found the Node launcher path above and other static child API
references whose focused reachability is unproved. This historical inventory is
not an exhaustive current transitive inventory or descendant-closure proof.
No new coverage producer, report or qualification was produced for this finding.

At the admission check, checkout and `/workspace/.vida-quality-inputs` shared the
same filesystem with 6,817,067,008 available bytes and 1,522,501 free inodes;
the observed bytes exceed the existing 2 GiB scratch floor. The exact retained
path `/workspace/.vida-retained` was absent. No cleanup or resource relocation
was performed, and this capacity observation does not clear the timing gate.

does not enable a trusted lane or alter any current acceptance gate. The CLI
observer delegates non-subject synchronous calls to the supplied fixture observer;
its own subjects still use the original native call and retain their own receipts.
This avoids both dropped direct-child observations and duplicate subject capture.

fixture observation, and `/workspace/contour-observer-target-8rxOf6` demonstrated

probe attempts `/workspace/contour-observer-target-lI7xlC` and `-mtHtfa` failed
because a nested Bun stdout write returned EPERM; those attempts remain retained.

## Trusted coverage specification decision, 2026-10-27

The owner approved separating trusted ordinary coverage from the physical
containment required for mutation and untrusted code. The verification contract
is in [TESTING](../../../packages/agent/TESTING.md#trusted-coverage-and-native-isolation);
the proposed execution steps are in the existing
[CLEAR plan](CLEAN-START-STRATEGY-20261009.md#trusted-coverage-implementation-plan).
This is a specification and reviewable plan, not a qualified implementation.
The active contour remains `bun-cli-run`; its existing full-proof HOLD, timing
gate and old UNKNOWN fences remain. No coverage run or numeric update was made
for this documentation change. Native DB, file, process and recovery contracts,
exact inventories, F/L/S/B and budgets remain required.

The earlier FD and bwrap probes below are retained as observations of the
existing route, not prerequisites that prove the new portable route or evidence
that it exists. Its launch-to-custody proof, platform capabilities and timing
margin must be established independently before any full successor. The owner
now prioritizes accepted Agent/Core contour closure over the earlier marketplace
resource priority; the current timer and schedule are unchanged.

An independent plan review found that the initial Task 2 lacked an actionable
effect-to-proof mapping. The plan now names the selected tests' fixture, SQLite,
socket, child, cache and dependency sites and proposes composing the existing
fixture-child and CLI observers under one owner, without a new IPC protocol.
This is a bounded source inspection, not a complete transitive effect inventory
or a proven portable implementation. The collector declares three run subjects;
TESTING now describes all three and the issue subject's prepared ancestry. This
documentation correction does not qualify historical receipts or add counters.

API impossibility. No permission escalation or retry was performed.

#### Objective disposition: `requireForwardFileLeaf` falsy arm

Per [TESTING objective coverage skips](../../../packages/agent/TESTING.md#objective-coverage-skips),
only this branch arm is dispositioned: bound report/run
`5627a2ea-26fe-40d3-ba4c-6002fc1ee729`, contour `bun-cli-run`, source
`packages/agent/bin/run.ts` SHA-256
`58b28b3a5adc9ca733579829de5e23975ab4c14a1542b959c43204ded135f591`, report
Source fingerprint `e365c5c58a27395a351afce83fc90a6688a3395da4d11b216b274beb6571b91c`,
test fingerprint `bd8115335b6142a813a15f68a1650e6d9fd7f0969b5807aa5fdaba90ef1604e8`,
and configured consumer context `agent-runtime.config.v1.yaml` digest
`d4d7c762c07dbb40bf89432548f9f117ebd99170bd756102c43e2a0af8decb97`, root
identity SHA-256
`af1a892ebc750f20732951994a999432dcc925546503fd7445f8ff829c051ddc`.
Selection is focused-diagnostic `tests/run-entrypoint.test.ts` and
`tests/bun-cache-routing.test.ts`; the shard exited 0, which is not full
contour qualification. Exact counter: branchMap key `58`, `if` at line 564,
columns 2–92, hits `[0,1]`; `requireForwardFileLeaf` functionMap key `54` at
line 563 has hit `1`. The mapped expression is
`if (!leaf) fail('GAP-VIDA-RUN-SELECTOR-001', 'Installed forward bundle path is invalid.');`.

The independent blind review
`DIAG/bun-cli-run-key412-objective-review-20261024-01.md` traced the sole caller
at line 1741 to `forwardUpdateAuthority`'s bound successor manifest. Every
entry is parsed through `parsePreparedPayloadEntry` and
`isPreparedRelativePath`, which requires a positive-length string and rejects
empty, `.` and `..` path components. Therefore `parts.at(-1)` is nonempty on
the supported validated route. `skipInstalledBundle` bypasses the loop rather
than creating a falsy leaf. A malformed internal map key would require bypassing
the supported manifest validator; exporting the private helper solely to inject
that value is not a behavior-level test. Root independently checked the exact
Source expression and validator/caller chain. This is an objective skip for
only the `!leaf` true/throw arm, not a missing fixture or broad function skip.

#### Key418 released-maintenance proof test

Key418 extends the existing owned-temp fixture for `blocks admission while the
selected cutover still holds maintenance`. With a schema-valid, digest-bound
released lock and receipt, it requires `assertNoActiveCutoverMaintenance` to
accept and then mutates each binding consumed by
`hasPreparedMaintenanceBindings`: quiescence attestation, activation path,
prepared plan record/schema/digest, archive manifest record/digest, and the
optional activation-decision digest. Every mutation retains the outer record
shape and rebinds the release hash, so the helper itself must reject the bad
binding. The fixture remains confined to its owned temporary directory and is
removed in `finally`; it performs no runtime, loader, database or consumer
effect.

#### Key424 historical inspection selection

`historicalReleaseInspection` is the next report-ordered zero callable. Its

supplied fallback. No duplicate selector was found. The packet exports this
small selector for the existing internal test import and adds a focused test
with distinct `current` and `supplied` records. Strict identity assertions
cover apply+supplied, inspect+supplied, and apply+undefined without a database,
consumer effect, or objective skip.

### Recent execution cost and order comparison

Measured producer times for keys410/411/412/413/414/415/416/417/418/419/420/421/422/423/424/425/426 were 182,091.09 /
196,291.21 / 176,802.72 / 184,266.83 / 185,087.10 / 191,756.33 /
202,630.39 / 187,589.41 / 191,494.56 / 215,386.40 / 207,589.09 /
208,864.42 / 182,574.22 / 188,314.45 / 181,773.73 / 197,219.28 /
225,461.82 ms. Each stage used different immutable image and source/test
inputs, so these figures do not compare ordering or parallelism causally.
Key416's focused target took 5,118.82 ms standalone and 6,514.59 ms in the
stage capture; those executions have different producer/test environments and
are not a controlled speed comparison.
Key420's focused target took 1,211 ms standalone; the 207,589 ms stage ran 117
tests with a different producer environment, so this is not an isolated
before/after comparison. Key421's focused denial target took 1,840 ms; its
208,864 ms stage also ran 117 tests, so these are not comparable timings.
Key422's focused target took 1,273 ms; its 182,574 ms stage ran 118 tests in
the producer image, so this is not a matched target-versus-stage comparison.
Key423 used static-first ordering (format, strict test typecheck, locked lint,
then focused target); its 1,235 ms target and 188,314 ms stage are not a
matched comparison to the earlier target-first packets because the source,
test inputs and immutable producer image differ.
Key424 kept static-first ordering; its focused target took 1,282 ms and the
stage ran 120 tests in 177.10 s. These values are also not causally comparable
with Key423 because the source/test/image inputs changed.
Key425 used static-first ordering with five callables in one focused test; its
1,189 ms target ran in a different producer image from the 197,219 ms stage
which selected 121 tests. The change in aggregate stage/test times is not a
controlled per-test setup cost or proof that packet size improved runtime.
Key426's target took 2,150 ms and the stage ran 122 tests in 219.45 s after
removing one redundant branch. The inputs and image changed, so neither the
longer aggregate test duration nor the one-branch denominator reduction is a
causal comparison or a qualification bypass.
Producer concurrency stayed at one. Observed order was focused target,
scoped formatting/strict/lint, independent review, ordinary hook-backed commit
and push, then one contour stage. A static-first order could have caught a
type issue before spending 9.85 s on the focused target; this is a savings
hypothesis, not a matched comparison (the prior Key415 hook caught a nullability
issue after target tests). Parallel width 2 for target plus strict/lint is
untested; the longest measured scoped gate is about 9.8 s lint, so possible
wall-time overlap is offset by contention risk and formatter must remain
exclusive. Width 1 remains the only measured producer setting and the chosen
test setting. An unfiltered run would repeat 105 unrelated tests for Key417;
that cost is not measured because no full suite ran. Fixture setup/runner
overhead are not separately reported; aggregate tool/API and token counts are
unavailable.
The tee-path mistakes are the confirmed capture inefficiency. Later attempts
and producer output have separate retained logs/receipts. No repository
test-script defect was established. `vex status` showed stale clusters, so only
bounded `rg` Source fallback was used; no index refresh or rebuild was started.

## Owners and acceptance boundaries

### Bun-host-state focused shard resource review

[TESTING](../../../packages/agent/TESTING.md#parallel-testing-current-status-and-required-contract)
and current focusedCoverageShardReview pins own eligibility/resources.
The [published original review](https://github.com/pomazanbohdan/vida-stack-lite/blob/1296dc6920c7ced34cf2fd59b6512bbc0084bcf3/.agent/work/core-runtime-reactivation/WORK.md#bun-host-state-focused-shard-resource-review)
preserves engineering provenance. Current1891/1899 reviews and1893pin control
cover this changed fixture packet, not other selections. Each selected file
retains its existing scratch namespace, hooks, clock rules and serial fallback.

[Sidecar](../../../AGENT.sidecar.md) owns repository/delegation policy;
[TESTING](../../../packages/agent/TESTING.md#rolling-contour-repair-cycle) owns gates;
[strategy](CLEAN-START-STRATEGY-20261009.md) owns order;
[backlog](../vida-013-planning-20261002/BACKLOG-PRIORITIES-20261006.md) owns stable
P0/P1/P2 categories. Product behavior belongs to
[system specification](../../../packages/agent/docs/system-specification.md#qualified-code-continuation-after-an-installed-update).
Root alone writes shared Source, owns Git and final numeric checks. Native blind
Luna receives bounded disjoint read/review outputs through Pstack contracts.
Superpowers debugging/verification preserves reproduced causes and fresh checks.

Static linkage, function execution, coverage, assertion, property and mutation
remain separate evidence. Generated scenarios stay unreviewed; N/A needs a cause.
Preserve exhaustive frozen correspondence, original CC/CRAP ceilings and
new-callable10/10 budgets. Only assertion-confirmed Killed counts; TIMEOUT,
compile/runtime errors, absent coverage and UNKNOWN never count.
No archived state replay, consumer effect, runtime/loader/DB cutover or team
unblock notice is authorized by this component coverage. Actual isolated-project
runtime/options/flows and native qualification remain a separate open task.

### Key448 objective disposition: non-Error catch arm ID138

A blind report-bound review found no supported CLI path that can make the
`error instanceof Error` conditional false. Inputs are JSON/filesystem records,
and the authority route owns its reader; it cannot receive a proxy/getter/callback
that throws a primitive. Project errors use `Error` or `fail()` (`RunCliError
extends Error`); parsers/proof helpers throw `Error`, while Node filesystem
errors are Error instances or translated by `fail()`. The selected fixtures
cover public leaf refusals, malformed records and cycle/bound guards. The
reviewer traced these throw sources under TESTING's causal prerequisite rule.
Evidence is `DIAG/bun-cli-run-key448-id138-review-20261024-01.md`.

result plus unchanged selector/cutoff bytes and Host database projection,
with no admission attempts before or after inspection. A rehashed release receipt over

inspection call, followed by exact lock/release/selector/journal/cutoff and Host
projection restoration checks.
